## 2026-10-27 Stage D118 — Verify real opened WAL slot identity, deny external hardlinks (RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **Verified WAL slot filesystem safety gap fixed:** Desktop WAL `safeFile` previously accepted hardlinks to arbitrary external files, and `adapter.read` first `lstat`ed the WAL slot but then used a NEW pathname lookup through `fs.readFile(path)`. A concurrent slot pathname replacement could redirect reading to the wrong external file between validation and use. `src/lib/v4/reencryption-desktop-wal.ts` now rejects `nlink !== 1` for `exists`, `read` and `write`; a WAL read opens an actual `FileHandle` and requires both fresh BigInt `lstat` and opened-handle `stat` to identify the same nonzero inode + device, be ordinary one-link files, and fit the 2 MiB limit BEFORE bounded descriptor reads. The descriptor read is bounded to 2 MiB plus overflow check even if the inode grows after stat, and always closes via `finally`. Additionally the exclusive lock handle uses read/write create-exclusive `wx+` and validates lock token and non-hardlinked inode via the SAME pinned handle (not a separate path reopen). Existing TS target remains unchanged; `BigInt(0)`/`BigInt(1)` rather than unsupported BigInt literals.
- **New real OS filesystem regressions:** An external private file hardlinked into WAL is rejected by `exists`, `read`, `write` and encrypted journal begin without changing the external file. If hardlink creation is blocked by the underlying volume/account, the test explicitly reports that environmental limitation. A hardlinked live exclusive WAL lock is also refused until the test-owned alias is removed, with token bytes preserved. A genuine encrypted slot still opens with descriptor-bound read; a subsequently oversized >2 MiB WAL slot refuses raw read and authenticated recovery.
- **Verification:** Desktop WAL focused **16/16**, full Fast **916/916**, Fast repeated **10×916/916** (exit 0), Recovery **80/80**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **136/136**, Maximum Strict TypeScript, production Build, E2E compile-only (three bundles), package and metadata validations, bounded 100,000,000-byte SHA-matching cryptographic Soak all PASS. Evidence: `docs/engineering/d118-locked-wal-descriptor-read-and-hardlink-refusal-2026-10-27.md`.
- **Scope limits / RELEASE BLOCKED:** This narrows final-slot TOCTOU but does NOT prove hostile parent-directory replacement is impossible, or establish a real vault-wide OS edit fence. Node-only Windows `fsync EPERM` still cannot certify physical power-loss durability. Crash-safe initiating-only Branch/Passphrase/Index cutover, recoverable new-key provenance, numeric-pin same-repo Source/Backup/Destination live GitHub acceptance and installed multi-device/mobile qualification still required. Owner D90/D91 topology unchanged; no Native Helper promotion, GitHub Actions, automatic old-branch deletion, policy waiver or Wizard enablement. Release gate remains `blocked`.

## 2026-10-27 Stage D115 — WAL directory-creation durability must include PARENT fsync (RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **Cross-platform durability gap fixed conservatively:** `src/lib/v4/reencryption-desktop-wal.ts` previously synced the WAL slot file and leaf directory, but not the parent-directory entries of newly created nested WAL directories. On an OS supporting leaf directory `fsync`, a successful leaf-only barrier could falsely imply that the newly created WAL directory itself survives a sudden power loss. The trusted absolute root is required to pre-exist; D115 now attempts `fsync` on every parent INSIDE that root, in order, after creating the directory tree and syncing the exclusive lock. Later leaf syncs count as durable only if this initial full parent chain succeeded too. Any unsupported directory sync (including Windows Node `EPERM`) remains fail-closed, and unexpected errors propagate. No Native Helper, protocol/WAL format, GitHub transport, release setting, or user Vault mutation was introduced.
- **Physical Windows filesystem regression:** `tests/v4/reencryption-desktop-wal.test.ts` adds newly created deeper `rekey/extra/nested/wal`, verifies encrypted journal readback and checks platform directory-sync support across the trusted root + all ancestor directories + leaf. If any platform directory sync is unsupported, `assertCrashDurable` must refuse to certify durability. The test does NOT simulate an actual physical power cut or prove macOS/Linux durability.
- **Verification:** Desktop WAL focused **11/11**, Fast **911/911**, Fast repeated **10×911/911** (exit 0), Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **136/136**, Max Strict TypeScript, production Build, GitHub E2E compile-only (3 bundles), package and metadata all PASS. Cryptographic soak **100,000,000 bytes**/12 chunks PASS with exact SHA-256. No credentialed sync/test-repository GitHub writes, installed Obsidian migration or real user Vault edits. Details: `docs/engineering/d115-rekey-wal-parent-directory-durability-2026-10-27.md`.
- **Remaining mandatory blocks:** Windows Node-only directory `fsync EPERM` still makes production WAL power-loss durability UNPROVEN. Supported-directory `fsync` by itself is not physical power-cut certification. Real cross-process initiating-vault writer exclusion, crash-safe initiating-only Branch/Passphrase/Index cutover, trusted new-key provenance, pinned numeric `GITHUB_E2E_EXPECTED_REPO_ID` same-repo three-branch GitHub E2E, installed Windows two-device/mobile qualification and independent release review remain open. Owner D90/D91 Source writable + Backup frozen + zero-parent Destination same-repo topology unchanged. Wizard remains Preview-only, `docs/release-gates/reencryption-wizard.json` `blocked`. No GitHub Actions, unapproved Native Helper, automatic cleanup or durability waiver.

## 2026-10-27 Stage D113 — Authenticated WAL adjacent generations MUST also preserve one valid state transition (RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **Real WAL ancestry gap found:** D108 rejected nonadjacent encrypted generations, but two individually authentic AES-GCM WAL slots with the **same key/run/source/destination and numerically adjacent generations** could still represent diverged histories (e.g. older has Blob A pending, newer confirms Blob B). Treating the higher generation as the next state would be unsafe.
- **Fixed `src/lib/v4/reencryption-recovery-journal.ts`:** `isAdjacentWalTransition` now enforces the complete older→newer single-operation state transition whenever BOTH slots authenticate: one new object Intent, matching object Receipt; one create-only ref Intent, matching verified ref Receipt; one-shot verified Backup→Staging; one-shot Candidate preparation SHA; or final prepared Candidate commit/tree/manifest Receipt. All unrelated fields are immutable between the two generations. Divergent paths/SHA/refs, unexpected removals or simultaneous jumps force **manual Recovery required**. D80 legacy standalone Blob staging remains supported. Neither observation nor CAS authorizes publication/cutover.
- **New regression:** Two independently generated valid encrypted WAL histories with gen-2 pending Git Blob A and gen-3 completed Git Blob B, valid MACs and identical WAL identity, are rejected by `load()`, `assertHealthySnapshot()` and further mutations. Valid adjacent upload/receipt state remains readable after restart. Journal focused **24/24**, Orphan publication **45/45**. Full Fast **906/906**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **136/136**, Maximum Strict TS, Build, GitHub E2E compile-only (3 bundles), package/metadata all PASS. No live GitHub writes, installed multi-device Obsidian migration or user vault changes.
- **Limits:** This is a two-slot **transition ancestry check**, NOT proof of power-loss durability, an unforgeable complete append-only history, or a real external OS write fence. Windows Node-only WAL directory `fsync EPERM` persists. Safe initiating-device-only Branch/Passphrase/Index Cutover, recoverable new-key provenance, real pinned `GITHUB_E2E_EXPECTED_REPO_ID` same-repo three-ref GitHub E2E and Windows two-device/mobile acceptance still needed. Release gate `blocked`, Wizard Preview-only. D90/D91 independent old Source / frozen Backup / zero-parent Destination policy unchanged, no GitHub Actions, unapproved Native Helper or durability waiver. ## 2026-10-27 Stage D112 — Encrypted post-commit prepared Candidate Receipt REQUIRED before Destination ref (INTERNAL; RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **D111 gap closed:** A one-shot *pre-commit* WAL marker existed before unreachable Git metadata Blob/tree/commit mutations, but successful creation of the actual zero-parent candidate **did not persist a separate post-creation Commit SHA/Tree SHA/final Manifest SHA-256 receipt**. A caller could otherwise offer a structurally plausible candidate from another run after restart.
- **`src/lib/v4/reencryption-recovery-journal.ts`:** Adds `V4ReencryptionPreparedCandidateReceipt` (exact `commitSha`, `treeSha`, `manifestSha256`) INSIDE the existing authenticated encrypted WAL, with one-shot generation-CAS `recordPreparedCandidateReceipt`. Requires sealed D111 preparation, completed staging, full Backup readback and no unresolved work; validates readback and requires Destination ref intent to match this receipt exactly. Older pre-release staging-only WAL remains readable.
- **`src/lib/v4/reencryption-ref-journal-coordinator.ts`:** After successful zero-parent Git Commit preparation and remote readback, records the D110 post-commit fingerprint + commit/tree, checks same OS WAL lease, both slots healthy and durability again. D102 Destination publication and D103 restart reconciliation demand exact matching prepared receipt; a WAL receipt alone NEVER authorizes publication, auto retry, Branch/Key Cutover or local writes. A simulated Git Commit completed but WAL receipt failed => pre-commit intent remains, no Destination ref and no blind preparation replay.
- **Evidence:** Focused encrypted Journal **22/22**, orphan publication **45/45**; full Fast **904/904**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **136/136**, Maximum Strict TS, Build, GitHub E2E compile-only (3 bundles), validate package/metadata PASS. New tests cover forged/missing receipt, exact manifest SHA, encrypted disk persistence, restart, post-commit receipt failure and no duplicate ref.
- **Remaining HARD BLOCKERS:** Node-only Windows WAL directory `fsync EPERM` power-loss durability UNPROVEN; crash-safe initiating-only Branch/Passphrase/Index Cutover, new-key provenance, real cross-process write fence and installed 2-device/mobile restore still pending. Live disposable GitHub three-ref E2E requires pinned numeric `GITHUB_E2E_EXPECTED_REPO_ID`; never bypass or guess. ## 2026-10-27 Stage D111 — WAL-sealed ONE-SHOT orphan candidate before Git metadata/tree/commit (RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **Finding:** D109 WAL ensured staging ciphertext Blob durability; D110 pinned final destination ref commit/tree/manifest, but the D96 orphan **metadata Blob, root Tree and zero-parent Commit Git POSTs happened BEFORE the destination ref-intent**. If GitHub accepted a metadata Blob and its response was lost, the process could attempt preparation again without any durable one-shot marker.
- **Fixed:** New `computeV4ReencryptionPreparationDigest` domain-separates the PRE-COMMIT immutable Source SHA/tree + new destination public KDF config + exact old Source config + new Device ID/Journal ID/createdAt + finalized file records + exact staged Git ciphertext receipts from the D110 POST-COMMIT digest. New encrypted two-slot WAL field `candidatePreparationSha256` and generation-CAS `recordCandidatePreparationIntent` persist a **single candidate-preparation intent before remote metadata Blob/Tree/Commit mutation**, require verified Backup + completed Staging, and forbid subsequent staging or repeated candidate preparation. Destination Ref Intent/readback now requires this phase. Old D80/D81 standalone WAL formats remain readable.
- **New guarded Coordinator `prepareV4JournaledOrphanCandidate`:** Checks the **same** OS WAL and exact Backup + all persisted receipts; seals one-shot pre-commit digest, demands crash-durable encrypted WAL and both healthy slots before each Git metadata Blob/tree/commit mutation. D102 Destination publisher and D103 restart reconciliation re-derive and require that pre-commit digest, in addition to D110's separate final Commit/Tree/manifest fingerprint. No automatic retry after ambiguous Git response, no local Cutover.
- **Evidence:** D111 tests assert empty-vault marker persistence, stale phase/Blob rejection, no duplicate candidate preparation after restart, unsupported Windows directory `fsync EPERM` **after sealing and before any candidate Git POST**, missing seal blocks Destination ref, and a GitHub mock which **accepts encrypted metadata Git Blob then loses HTTP response** is not retried. Real disposable Windows WAL two-slot readback retains encrypted marker; full Fast **902/902**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **136/136**, max strict TS, build, E2E compile-only (3), package/metadata all PASS. No credentialed real GitHub mutation, installed two-device/mobile Obsidian or physical power-loss certification in this stage.
- **Production hard blockers:** D86 Node-only Windows directory `fsync EPERM` is NOT resolved; genuine power-loss WAL durability, trusted cross-process Vault edit fence, durable initiating-only Branch/Passphrase/Index Cutover, secret provenance and live GitHub three-branch acceptance pinned to verified numeric `GITHUB_E2E_EXPECTED_REPO_ID` are still missing. The protected wrapper uses a structural OS WAL interface; mock success is not proof. Release gate `docs/release-gates/reencryption-wizard.json` stays **blocked**; Settings Wizard remains Preview-only, D90/D91 independent old Source + frozen old-key Backup + new encrypted orphan Destination unchanged; no GitHub Actions or unapproved Native Helper. ## 2026-10-27 Stage D110 — Encrypted Destination commit/manifest/KDF fingerprint across publish AND restart Recovery (RELEASE BLOCKED)

Deterministic across JS object-property order and list order; hashes one record/receipt at a time. No note plaintext, passphrases or derived secret keys are persisted. WAL stores **only fingerprint inside AES-GCM encrypted payload**, never as unencrypted journal metadata.

Both journal and coordinator `sameRef` comparisons include the fingerprint, so changing a plaintext SHA-256, Blob SHA or PBKDF2 salt when rebuilding recovery evidence leaves the original WAL intent pending and **fails before GitHub ref mutation or remote readback**. Full immutable SHA/content verification still mandatory; digest is NOT a cutover authorization or independent provenance proof.

Existing D80 standalone staging-only use remains supported.

Updated D101/D103 on-disk tests to use exact three-branch handoff. ## 2026-10-27 Stage D107 — Double-pass physical initiating-vault SHA-256 against post-hash file changes (INTERNAL; RELEASE BLOCKED)

- **Found a real D105/D106 TOCTOU proof gap:** After hashing a local note once, another program could change it while later notes are still being hashed, then restore the same size and `mtime`. The old final inventory-only check could miss an already-hashed but now-modified note because the D106 handle was already closed. **This was not a fully atomic snapshot.**
- **Fixed `src/lib/v4/reencryption-initiator-parity.ts`:** D105 now performs two complete independent bounded streaming SHA-256 passes over every authenticated pinned-source local file, comparing all hash/size/byte totals, whole-scope physical inventory and live Sync/editor/credentials/lease guards between and after passes. D100/D102 destination publication uses D105, so the stricter check applies before GitHub ref publication without a new integration point.
- **Tests:** New D107 same-size/same-`mtime` mutation AFTER first file's hash but during later file read is correctly rejected on pass two. New disposable REAL Windows filesystem regression also injects an external edit after a prior file's first SHA. ## 2026-10-26 Stage D90 — FINAL OWNER POLICY: independent re-encrypted branch FORK, no fleet-wide migration

- **Owner's authoritative decision** after reviewing alternatives: Re-encryption **forks an immutable pinned source snapshot** into a **NEW orphan branch with genuinely new encrypted content and new branch-specific configuration**, not a Git history clone and not a synchronized fleet migration. The initiating device selects and verifies the fork; only **after successful and recoverable destination verification** may that device switch itself to new branch/new credentials.
- **All OTHER clients are independent:** may continue syncing **old branch forever**, even while/after another device forks. Following the new branch is entirely optional and manual (new branch and passphrase), never automatic. Source and destination diverge with no automatic merge or catch-up; warn about intentional divergence and unresolved local changes. **NO** registered-device acknowledgement, old/offline client freeze, fleet-wide sync pause, migration-specific pre-sync blocking, central migration marker, or forced device enrollment.
- **Snapshot boundary:** Source data must come consistently from one exact authenticated immutable Git commit. Subsequent valid old-branch commits do NOT invalidate an already selected snapshot or block old-branch Sync. Initiating-device local snapshot/cutover must still detect concurrent local edits safely; this does not imply a remote old-branch lock. Preserve all old/backup branches until explicit user deletion.
- **Pre-Sync:** ordinary safety checks remain in all normal Sync triggers (branch-ref equality and actual commit type, remote config/KDF compatibility, recovery/conflict guard, publication CAS). A new re-encrypted branch is **not** itself a reason to block Sync on any existing old branch. An abandoned uncommitted migration-specific Pre-Sync Guard experiment was withdrawn; `src/lib/v4/reencryption-preflight.ts` no longer demands other-device pause or a migration marker. `src/lib/github-api.ts` was independently hardened to fail closed on an unexpected ref or non-commit object even if GitHub returns HTTP 200.

Re-encryption Wizard first-release gate remains **blocked** until real fork implementation, verified backup/restore, WAL durability and platform acceptance.

## 2026-10-24 Stage D80 — encrypted two-slot journal for orphan re-encryption upload intent/receipts (NOT wired to live migration)

- **Base/worktree:** from clean/pushed D79 `7486358`, isolated `.worktrees/d80-rekey-recovery-journal` branch `audit/2026-10-24-d80-rekey-recovery-journal`. Root/master, D63 owner WIP, old audit worktrees and real vault untouched. No GitHub Actions, no actual remote migration branch, user data mutation, automatic cutover or release.
- **Gap addressed:** D79’s all-file stream stage/receipt manifest is ephemeral; after crash or ambiguous GitHub Blob response, an unreachable blob might have been created, but no safe receipt can be inferred. New `src/lib/v4/reencryption-recovery-journal.ts` provides **encrypted two-slot locally pluggable WAL** via existing `V4LocalIndexAdapter`. Each AES-GCM journal payload authenticated with destination journalKey and AAD bound to destination repoId, pinned old source commit SHA, run ID and generation. Header SHA-256 catches torn/corrupt writes; do not confuse this with unauthenticated plaintext hash replacing AES-GCM. No plaintext logical paths, keys, tokens or opaque Git object paths/SHAs in plaintext header. Bounded to 2 MiB; deliberately fail-closed above cap. Different namespace from production Sync WAL.
- **Intent vs receipt:** `begin()` only on fully absent slots; `recordUploadIntent(opaquePath/expectedGitSHA/byteLength,expectedGeneration)` **must be durably committed BEFORE actual Git Blob upload**; `appendReceipt()` only after exact intent matches. `load()` always returns snapshots with `requiresRemoteRevalidation:true`, `permissionToPublish:false`, `cutoverAuthorized:false`. Power loss between intent and result leaves a pending intent, NOT a completed receipt. Torn newest slot may roll back to last authenticated slot but further mutation blocks until manual reconciliation; both invalid slots raise `V4ReencryptionJournalRecoveryRequiredError`. Malformed/stale/duplicate entries or wrong key/source/dest identity fail closed. Single-instance busy guard implemented; **cross-process exclusive lock still MISSING**.
- **Regression evidence:** `tests/v4/reencryption-recovery-journal.test.ts` **8/8 PASS**: two-slot restart, raw slot redaction, missing/corrupt latest/dual slots, wrong key/runId/source/dest, stale generation and invalid receipts, synthetic torn power-loss write, pending upload intent restart, mismatch refusing to clear intent, no reset/publish/cutover operations. Frozen offline install PASS, maximum strict `pnpm build` PASS, full fast/resource/recovery/feasibility suites PASS, E2E three-bundle compile PASS, metadata/package validation PASS. Docs: `docs/engineering/d80-encrypted-rekey-recovery-journal-2026-10-24.md`.
- **DO NOT claim production crash safety yet:** This is a *journal protocol/prototype*, **not wired** to D79’s `stageImmutableObject` or any platform/native vault implementation. `V4LocalIndexAdapter.write` does NOT guarantee platform fsync/power-loss durability or exclusive process locking; those require separate implementation and real OS/mobile acceptance. Neither returned receipts nor journal certify stored Git objects or a verified backup: must re-read by exact SHA, reconcile 404/timeout ambiguity, verify complete destination tree, and independently restore on second device. Needs WAL capacity/paging, secure error UX and old-object cleanup policy.
- **Owner decision boundary:** Approved A: orphan root on new branch in same physical repo; retain old source for recovery and verify second device before cutover. Owner has **NOT** approved whether old-branch clients must pause Sync throughout migration/cutover. No automatic freeze, reconcile or cutover silently chosen.

Next safe work: secure platform journal adapter, trusted upload intent+receipt integration into D79, Git immutable object remote readback and backup proof, then native multi-device acceptance.

## 2026-10-24 Stage D77 — ciphertext-aware 8 MiB writer budget for normal Sync + Re-encryption

- **Branch/worktree:** `audit/2026-10-24-d77-ciphertext-size-budget` at `.worktrees/d77-ciphertext-size-budget`, forked from clean pushed D76 `fb2f9cb`. Primary checkout, D63 user WIP, and all other audit worktrees untouched. No GitHub Actions, no real GitHub source/test branch mutations, no release/cutover.
- **Bug fixed:** V4 writer classified/split **plaintext** at 8 MiB even when AES-GCM adds a 33-byte overhead, causing single encrypted objects or parts at the limit to exceed the fixed 8 MiB GitHub REST-safe *ciphertext object* cap. New `maxV4NewWriterPlaintextBytes(mode)` subtracts overhead only for encrypted mode. `shouldWriteV4Parts(bytes,mode)` now classifies by actual on-wire object; default plaintext remains legacy compatible. `V4StorageCodec.prepare` / `prepareParts` / `prepareFromSource` now choose chunking and cap each part correctly; stream objectCount/paths and part read loop use the same effective size. Normal `sync-session` passes encryption mode to planner and `selectV4WriterPartBytes`, which accounts for ciphertext overhead and mutation headroom. D76 transcode removes previous narrow near-limit rejection and safely chunks instead. **8 MiB policy has NOT been increased**; old 48 MiB part reads still supported.
- **Fail-first regression evidence:** `tests/v4/ciphertext-size-budget.test.ts` started **4/4 RED**, finished **4/4 GREEN** after fix (threshold ±1, real AES-GCM encrypted single/chunked round trip, streaming requested 8 MiB clamped to safe plaintext part size, policy). Updated transcode suite **8/8 PASS**, including newly allowed near-8 MiB file. Added normal real production `V4SyncSession` in-memory encrypted Force Push regression at `8 MiB - 33 + 1` bytes; no ciphertext blob exceeds 8 MiB, result stores as chunked; focused Sync tests **94/94 PASS**. Initial whole-file read for scan hashing is expected at this size, but upload itself uses bounded streaming.
- **Resource contract updated:** virtual 5 GiB streamed **writer** previously asserted 107 parts at the old 48 MiB legacy-reader size; now accurately asserts 640 at fixed 8 MiB. No 5 GiB physical file was allocated or read; owner physical file test limit <=100 MB preserved. Resource suite PASSED after this intentional expectation update.
- **Quality:** `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` PASS; maximum strict `pnpm build` PASS; full `pnpm test:fast` PASS (pre-final Sync regression; focused Sync 94/94 PASS after), `pnpm test:resource` PASS, `pnpm test:recovery` PASS, `pnpm test:feasibility` PASS, GitHub E2E three-bundle compile PASS, metadata/package validators PASS. Details: `docs/engineering/d77-ciphertext-size-budget-2026-10-24.md`.
- **Remaining critical release gates:** No entire-vault re-encryption or verified independent backup/WAL recovery/second physical device/Wizard GUI/per-device cutover implemented. D66 `reencryption-wizard.json` still `blocked`; one topology check passed, 6 pending. Owner old-branch device Sync freeze policy remains UNANSWERED; DO NOT infer approval. Next safe work: trusted inventory-wide output verification / backup plan / exact commit readback without a migration publication.

## 2026-10-24 Stage D76 — source→destination bounded encrypted object transcode (NO publish)

- **Worktree/branch:** from clean/pushed D75 `3625d67`, isolated `.worktrees/d76-rekey-stream-transcode`, branch `audit/2026-10-24-d76-rekey-stream-transcode`. Root/master, owner D63 worktree, other audit branches and real user vault untouched. No GitHub Actions, GitHub/live E2E mutation, release or actual migration in this stage.
- **New code:** `src/lib/v4/reencryption-stream-transcode.ts` exports `createV4ReencryptedObjectStream`, a pure per-file **V4 content re-encryption preparation** helper. Validates approved D74 same-repository orphan plan, encrypted V4 source/destination repoId, fresh KDF salt, different path/content keys, bounded new KDF iterations, authenticated record path/object HMAC shape, non-dirty/non-deleted record, safe target version and source pinned commit. Old single/pack AES-GCM content is decoded through original `V4StorageCodec`; old chunked parts use per-part source AAD and **sequential decryption** (no whole logical-file concat). New `V4StorageCodec.prepareFromSource` streams new AES-GCM ciphertext with cryptorandom fresh opaque **fileId**, pathId and object coordinates; old pack refs become independent new single/chunked refs. Callback must read immutable object at EXACT pinned SHA and independently verify source HEAD. No GitHub, vault, settings, key rotation or ref I/O in helper.
- **IMPORTANT:** The helper yields **potentially unverified** immutable destination objects while streaming; caller must consume ALL and successfully `finalize()` and check all records/global inventory **before any index/head/ref publication**. Final hash and size are checked by production stream codec. D76 discovered near-8 MiB plaintext single-blob **ciphertext overhead** boundary (8 MiB plaintext + 33 bytes exceeds REST max). For plaintext size in `(8MiB - AES overhead, 8MiB]`, D76 fails closed until generic codec threshold can be fixed. New stream encrypted parts use **8 MiB minus overhead** bytes to keep actual remote payload <= 8 MiB. Legacy packed-object reader still uses whole pack decode; large-pack resource risk remains. Existing 8 MiB part policy unchanged.

## 2026-10-24 Stage D72 — bind pre-rekey Git reader to physical repository identity

- **Base/worktree:** Isolated `audit/2026-10-24-d72-rekey-repository-identity` at `.worktrees/d72-rekey-repository-identity`, based on clean/pushed D71 `f4ada99`. - **Finding:** D70–D71 exact-commit/crypto guard authenticated `repoId` from remote config and a named Git ref, but did **not** prove the underlying **physical GitHub repository ID**. An unrelated/mis-scoped client with a copied config, a repository transfer/rename or client destination switch could pass if branch/object commit and config matched.
- **Safe solution:** `GitHubClient.getRepositoryIdentity()` adds an authenticated read-only `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` returning validated GitHub numeric `id` and `full_name` (fail closed on 404/403, bad IDs, malformed names). `verifyGitHubReencryptionSource()` now requires this method and matches the physical repo full name against expected `repoId` owner/name (case-insensitive for GitHub), then records its numeric ID **before any Git ref or object I/O**; re-reads after D71 exact-tree/blob, AES-GCM and SHA-256 verification and fails on repository ID/name movement. No change to normal sync code path or D66 release gate. It does **not** solve source-ref ABA, later changes, or persist owner-approved repo numeric IDs across runs.
- **Verification:** Added fail-first `tests/v4/github-repository-identity.test.ts` (RED absent method → **2/2 GREEN**) and two new `tests/v4/reencryption-trusted-loader.test.ts` tests (physical identity mismatch blocks before any ref/tree, ID changes mid-verification refused); full focused loader **12/12 GREEN**. Frozen offline install PASS; strict TypeScript `pnpm build` PASS; `pnpm test:fast` PASS; `pnpm test:resource` PASS; `pnpm test:recovery` PASS; `pnpm test:feasibility` PASS; GitHub E2E compile 3 bundles PASS; metadata/package checks PASS. No live GitHub or physical/native two-device testing was carried out for this newly unconnected reader. Full limitations: `docs/engineering/d72-reencryption-physical-repository-identity-2026-10-24.md`.

Added realistic Obsidian slider stubs + tests for visible policy, exact 600k–1.2m boundaries, one Save per double click, failed-save dirty state.
- **Durable boundary:** `saveSettings` validates before side effects, reserves new secret IDs for rotated token/passphrase, writes new candidate secrets, persists sanitized candidate settings, and only then publishes `plugin.settings`, invalidates future keyring generation, reinitializes GitHub/schedules. Old IDs are scrubbed with empty secret values only after durable commit (Obsidian `SecretStorage` has **no deleteSecret API**). In-flight sync sessions retain pinned connection/KDF/settings snapshot. Candidate writes rejected by `saveData` are treated as **ambiguous**: exact matching readback is required for safe reconciliation; if readback is not conclusive, keep candidate secrets to protect a possibly committed record and surface the failure without changing active settings. SecretStorage-write failure before file persist scrubs only pending candidate IDs best effort.
- **Native harness correction:** `scripts/run-obsidian-live-github-ui.mjs` and `scripts/obsidian-live-ui-peer.mjs` previously mutated live `p.settings` before `await p.saveSettings()`; they now call `await p.saveSettings(nextSettings)` on a staged copy. This ensures native test setup exercises the new atomic visibility boundary.
- **Regression and local gates:** Focused Settings tests RED→GREEN including delayed disk write, rejection, SecretStorage old-ID preservation, candidate readback after lost save acknowledgment, UI policy and Save error; `pnpm build`/strict TypeScript exit 0; full `pnpm test:fast` exit 0; resource **11/11**, recovery exit 0, feasibility **134/134**, three GitHub E2E bundles compile, metadata/package validators exit 0, `git diff --check` exit 0. No feature rekey/migration, no Force Push/Pull automation, no release. **Native exact-source verification must be recorded after committing the code SHA**, including pinned disposable GitHub branch absence verification and cleanup.
- **D65 committed-code native acceptance PASS:** Exactly committed clean code SHA `02dab27f8afa777037822fc5d6e9dee5966150fa` (not an uncommitted build) ran on real Windows Obsidian two isolated instances with the revised `saveSettings(nextSettings)` harness. Multi-mutation WAL A has durable stage-write receipt, B is still pending. (1) If B is user-edited to same-sized bytes after scan and B synthetic stat seam returns old mtime, SHA-256 precondition blocks overwrite and saves `replan-required`, but A's verified backup and stage were already finalized/deleted. (2) If B stage is absent, A's verified backup/stage are finalized first, then B stage open fails ENOENT; B original bytes remain, WAL stays `local-committing` with A receipt. (3) If remote HEAD mismatches WAL's verified HEAD, replay records `replan-required` with **zero native finalization and no backup/stage cleanup**. These are behavior characterizations: **D54–D57 multi-mutation partial-replay non-atomicity is not fixed.**

Node process peak RSS **251,531,264 bytes**. ## 2026-10-24 Stage D47 — read-only branch-head diagnostics on terminal test failures

- **Isolated ledger-owned D47:** `.worktrees/d47-branch-ref-forensics`, `audit/2026-10-24-d47-branch-ref-forensics`, directly from D46 exact-SHA native/live evidence docs commit `5d9a1d6`. - **Test-only missing-head forensics:** `tests/github-e2e/support/pre-publish-head-null-evidence.ts` and test-only wiring into `tests/github-e2e/v4-real-github-e2e.test.ts`. On an otherwise-terminal **pre-publish, not-published, expected valid commit SHA, observedHeadSha=null** publication error, independently perform read-only branch-ref and expected immutable-commit observations; log only statuses, validated SHA identities and device/local base metadata. ## 2026-10-23 Stage D30 — reject duplicate Git tree object paths before blob upload (Node.js)

- **Isolated branch/source:** Started at verified D28 SHA `4121151ec676594a93ce61e7b0b4c53e62a13104` in `.worktrees/d30-git-duplicate-paths`, branch `audit/2026-10-23-d30-git-tree-duplicate-paths`. Concurrent D29 remote-head validation and D25 GUI/telemetry worktrees were not modified. User's Zed root, personal vault, test credentials and other worktrees untouched.
- **RED:** `uploadV4TreeFiles` allowed two distinct Git blobs with the same remote tree path. The resulting `fileShas` keyed map silently overwrote the first SHA and both same-path tree entries reached `createGitTree`. A deterministic regression requiring fail-closed before blob/network writes failed (focused **14/15 PASS, 1 FAIL: Missing expected rejection**).
- **Fix:** Check the `input.files` tree paths for duplicates in one pass before progress callbacks or any Git blob creation; reject with explicit `Duplicate V4 Git tree object path` error. This retains legitimate many-logical-file shared packs represented as **one** Git object with several progress items. No changes to remote format, encrypted bytes, CAS/reconciliation, WAL, conflicts, retry policy or protocol.

## 2026-10-23 Stage D28 — Reject duplicate encrypted-pack file IDs before publication

- **Isolated scope:** Worktree `.worktrees/d28-duplicate-pack-fileids`, dedicated branch `audit/2026-10-23-d28-duplicate-pack-fileids`, base D27 `e620342ad4e9c980cceb3629eb54d52436ae8ef9`. Main Zed checkout and concurrent D25/D26/D27 worktrees unchanged.
- **Data-integrity root cause / RED:** Encrypted V4 pack archives are JSON maps indexed by `fileId`. Two distinct records with the same `fileId` could be accepted by either `V4StorageCodec.preparePack` (in-memory) or `preparePackFromSources` (streaming), producing ambiguous duplicate JSON keys or silently overwriting an entry via `Object.fromEntries`; the second record could reference bytes that do not match its plaintext hash. Added deterministic two-path malformed-input test first: focused **7/8 PASS, 1 FAIL** because no rejection was raised.
- **Minimal fix:** Shared `assertUniqueV4PackFileIds` checks both producers before assembling the JSON archive. Duplicate IDs reject with an explicit error, preventing construction/upload of an inconsistent pack. No change to valid pack ciphertext format, fileId generation, conflict semantics, WAL, GitHub CAS, scheduling or keys. Defense in depth; no evidence that ordinary planner emits duplicate IDs.
- **GREEN local verification:** Focused **8/8**, build PASS, tracked Fast **514/514**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **132/132**, virtual bounded **100,000,000-byte soak 1/1**, GitHub E2E three-bundle compile PASS, metadata and package validation PASS. Fast repeat **10×514/514 PASS**, with no intermittent failures.
- **Scope and release caution:** Node.js+Obsidian API primary; GitHub Issue #9 (native desktop OS TOCTOU research) remains deliberately deferred. Did not touch original vault, real GitHub API, release/master/GitHub Actions, native helper or physical files >100MB. Latest-code actual two-instance Obsidian GUI acceptance, cross-device C2 format, line-merge UX, mobile/macOS/Linux and independent security review are distinct pending release gates.

## 2026-10-23 Stage D27 — HTTP Retry-After date and long-timer safety (Node.js)

- **Isolated source:** Base is audited/pushed D26 `4f39c054b38614760fcb016ec0676bd7eae7b301`. Worktree `.worktrees/d27-retry-after-http-date`, distinct `audit/2026-10-23-d27-retry-after-date` branch. Existing Zed root, concurrent D25/D26 worktrees, user vault and secrets untouched.
- **RED (real deterministic defects):** `resolveV4RateLimitDelay` treated standard HTTP-date `Retry-After` as unparseable and substituted the shorter 60,000ms policy fallback rather than the actual 120,000ms server deadline (focused regression failed, `60000 !== 120000`). Separately, `V4RequestScheduler` forwarded deadlines longer than signed 32-bit timer range to Node/Electron `setTimeout`; a second fail-first test rejected the unsafe >2,147,483,647ms delay.
- **Fix:** Honor the existing numeric-second `Retry-After` form; also parse dated values with `Date.parse`, preserving exact non-negative remaining time. Empty/invalid headers fall through to existing reset/fallback handling. Split long policy waits into at most 2,147,483,647ms chunks and re-check the original cooldown/pacing deadline after each chunk. No changes to GitHub mutation spacing/concurrency/retry count, protocol, WAL, conflict handling, key material or UI.
- **GREEN evidence:** Focused **8/8** after both RED cases; `pnpm build` PASS; `pnpm test:fast` **513/513** PASS; `pnpm test:repeat` **10×513/513** PASS; recovery **79/79**, resource **11/11**, feasibility **132/132**, virtual bounded **100,000,000-byte soak 1/1**, three GitHub E2E bundle compile, metadata and package checks all PASS. These are local tests; no live GitHub roundtrip or real Obsidian acceptance is claimed for D27.
- **Safety and remaining qualification:** GitHub Issue #9 native filesystem TOCTOU research stays open and **DEFERRED**; Node.js and Obsidian API remain primary. No GitHub Actions, `master`, release, native binary, user vault, or physical test file over 100 MB. Latest-code actual Obsidian GUI acceptance, physical mobile/macOS/Linux, C2 cross-device format coordination and independent release/security review remain separate gates.

No protocol or adapter contract change for normally awaited sequential callers. Stage errors remain fail-closed with best-effort remove; no stage receipt returned when a write fails. Valid zero-byte stages still supported.

## 2026-10-23 Stage D18 — Native File ID / WAL identity binding and strict-share PoC (NOT integrated)

- **Physical fail-first bug:** D17's pinned FILE HANDLE + repeated content SHA did **not** verify the object remained at its expected logical filename. During the `READY` barrier, an external process renamed `original.md` to an unrelated user path and replaced the former name with a symlink; old D17 helper still moved the user-renamed object into transaction backup. D18 regression initially **RED**.
- **Mitigation in TEST-ONLY PoC:** `poc/windows-handle-no-follow/RenameProbe.cs` now reads Win32 `GetFileInformationByHandleEx(FileIdInfo=18)`: full 128-bit File ID + volume serial; `capture` mode writes both original/stage IDs and expected SHA hashes into **fsync'd synthetic D18 WAL intent v2**. Every fresh helper/rollback rechecks the WAL file IDs after the native rename; permissive commit/rollback reopens the expected logical filename **relative to a pinned directory handle** and compares ID with the held source immediately before rename. Real symlink and byte-identical different-file-ID swaps now **GREEN** and preserve user notes/backup. This is a research-only WAL fixture, **not production WAL or authenticated evidence**.
- **Demonstrated residual vulnerability:** Another physical test deliberately pauses **after File ID/name comparison and before rename**, then swaps the logical name. Permissive mode still incorrectly moves the user-renamed file object. **10/10 reproduced UNSAFE** with a protocol pause; never claim OS TOCTOU resolved. File IDs alone are not an atomic name lock.
- **Alternative strict file-share RESEARCH mode:** `commit-locked` (and `commit-locked-gap`) retains Windows FILE HANDLEs with `FILE_SHARE_READ` only (no share-write/delete). Windows NT `10.0.26300` x64 local test: late foreign rename/write attempts returned `EBUSY` even during forced proof-to-rename gap, and helper committed safely. If an external app had already opened the note for writing, native lock open refused **before mutation**. This has significant compatibility/UX tradeoffs: **do not silently enable in released plugin**; user must approve broad external-write-handle defer/retry behavior separately from the existing Obsidian open-note policy 1.
- **Evidence accounting:** `node scripts/test-windows-native-rename-poc.mjs --repeat=10`: **26 cases ×10 = 260 observations** = **250 protective PASS** in 25 categories + **10 expected unsafe gap demonstrations** in 1 category. Exit 0 explicitly reports `POC_COMPLETE_WITH_PROVEN_RESIDUAL_RACE`. D16 separate handle-delete PoC **8/8 PASS**. Build, Fast **503/503**, Repeat **10×503/503**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **132/132**, virtual bounded 100 MB soak **1/1**, metadata/package and GitHub E2E compile-only PASS. D18 code confined to manual research PoC and harness; production `src/`, `package.json`, native release artifacts unchanged.
- **Documentation:** `docs/engineering/windows-handle-file-id-share-race-d18-2026-10-23.md` contains real threat proof, Microsoft API references, strict-sharing UX options and residual risk. D14 native Windows encrypted 1000-note proof/D15 small native remain last product integration acceptance. No master, GitHub Actions, actual vault, binary distribution or platform choice.

After process termination immediately after each of four native rename boundaries (backup, publish, park, restore), a **fresh child process** proof-gated rollback restores the complete original, retaining staged candidate or verified replacement residue. External sentinel never modified; synthetic vaults and compiled `.exe` all deleted under ignored `.tmp`. No native binary tracked, no GitHub write or personal vault.

## 2026-10-23 Stage D15 — close late async cleanup junction swaps (OS-handle TOCTOU remains)

- **Risk:** After async hash-proving original/staged bytes, Desktop `finalizeStage` could call `await fs.rm(original-backup)` without rechecking parent path containment. `rollbackStage` likewise could remove plugin replacement residue after the last asynchronous original-content proof. Another process could install a junction to outside the vault during the yielded gap.
- **Physical Windows fail-first:** Two new real NTFS/junction regressions in `tests/v4/staged-desktop-io-containment.test.ts` temporarily intercept Node promise `stat` at the **last proof step** and atomically swap the parent into an outside junction. Prior unguarded code returned success despite the swap (**RED**, 2 tests). Narrow fix uses the D12 synchronous `assertRecoveryPathsSync` right before `rmSync(backup/residue)`, with **no JS await** between proof re-check and deletion. Both cases now reject unsafe paths, preserving local backups/residue and external sentinel bytes (**GREEN**, 14/14 containment).
- **Acceptance:** Build, Fast **503/503**, Repeat Fast **10×503**, Recovery **79/79**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **132/132**, virtual bounded 100 MB soak **1/1**, metadata/package checks and E2E compile 3 bundles PASS. Details at `docs/engineering/desktop-late-cleanup-d15-2026-10-23.md`. **Native PASS**: On exact D15 code SHA `dc23dce35688bf7a777630d4978867b5f1478f55`, Windows Obsidian 1.14.4 encrypted **two-instance small journey** passed (real GitHub opaque ciphertext, peer Force Pull restore, Unicode/rename/delete/recreate, preserved two-device conflicting revisions, no-op, 0 UI exceptions, test branch deleted/absence verified, both managed processes cleaned). Peer download wall **8,399ms**. This is a small smoke journey; full 1,000-note D14 acceptance on prior SHA remains separately valid, not rerun for D15.
- **Critical residual:** Path `lstatSync` + `rmSync` are distinct OS syscalls; a genuinely concurrent process can still swap between them. Full directory-handle-relative no-follow API requires separately approved platform-specific architecture, especially Windows; do **not** claim fixed or production-ready. Network Drive policy B, Recovery A, open-note policy 1, pinned settings policy B unchanged. No `master`, release/tag, personal vault or GitHub Actions.

## 2026-10-23 Stage D11 — real Windows child-process crash boundaries; rollback proof-gated completion

- **Scope:** Isolated audit worktree `.tmp/staged-production-6c67c8a`, base commit `01a7ce4266d4f4051974a2a2a6bfb4a27f4e1503`. No change to `master`, no GitHub Actions, no personal vault. User policy 1 for open notes and credential settings policy B remain unchanged.
- **Detailed evidence:** `docs/engineering/desktop-crash-boundary-d11-2026-10-23.md`. Added auto-discovered `tests/recovery/staged-process-kill.test.mjs` and `tests/recovery/support/staged-process-kill-child.ts`. They bundle real production platform/recovery code into a *disposable CommonJS child process*, patch its own `fs.renameSync` to exit immediately **after the native rename returned**, inspect actual disk files/WAL, then launch a separate new process for rollback/recovery. All test fixtures live inside the audit worktree `.tmp/native-kill-d11-*` and are cleaned up.
- **Additional WAL receipt fail-first:** A real staged commit previously deleted its original backup **before** the caller durably saved the WAL receipt. New fail-first test reproduced unsafe early cleanup. D11 retains backup until the receipt is saved and then calls proof-gated `finalizeStage()`. The direct non-WAL large-file path explicitly finalizes on success to avoid leaving an orphan backup. A child exits after `commitStage()` returns but before receipt, and a fresh child either replays the remote candidate or discards and restores original bytes, both PASS.
- **External process termination PASS:** Added `tests/recovery/staged-external-termination.test.mjs` + preload-only `tests/recovery/support/staged-external-terminate-preload.cjs`. A separate parent sends external `SIGKILL`/Windows TerminateProcess after the child emitted a post-rename handshake while blocked with `Atomics.wait`. All four commit/rollback boundaries then recover correctly with a fresh child; the external-kill suite passed **5 further consecutive runs** in addition to the initial PASS. This is stronger than an internal `process.exit()` but still not a power failure inside the syscall. See `docs/engineering/desktop-os-termination-d11-2026-10-23.md` for the independent evidence and limits.
- **Four real termination boundaries PASS:** commit after target→original-backup, commit after stage→target, rollback after target→replacement-residue, rollback after original-backup→target. Verified exact original/candidate bytes, durable journal slots, stage/backup/residue states, correct receipt and cleanup where applicable.
- **Fail-first root cause:** The final rollback cut originally failed because the original had been safely restored and the backup no longer existed but staged residue remained. Former code always demanded Manual Recovery. D11 narrowly closes this case: if and only if the restored target passes original WAL size/mtime/SHA-256 proof and the plugin residue passes staged size/SHA-256 proof, perform a second original proof check and remove **only** the residue. Otherwise preserve both files and require manual review. Negative tests cover changed original and changed residue.
- **Additional fail-first root cause and fix:** Desktop `commitStage` used to delete the original `.target-backup` **before WAL receipt persistence**. A new proof-gated regression failed before the change and passed after retaining the backup until `applyV4RecoveryLocalMutations` saves the receipt and calls `finalizeStage`. The direct non-WAL large Pull path now calls `finalizeStage` explicitly **before** reporting mutation completion (extra fail-first `tests/v4/sync-session.test.ts` verifies `commit → finalize → completed`). The child-process harness adds two after-success-before-receipt crashes (discard/restored original and resume/staged target), and three adversarial fresh-process tampering cases that all preserve bytes+WAL and demand manual review. These do **not** imply fsync/power-loss qualification.
- **Verification:** Focused real-process kill PASS; staged filesystem regressions 17/17 PASS; Production Build, full Fast, Recovery, 10x Repeat Fast, Feasibility, Resource, bounded 100,000,000-byte virtual crypto soak, metadata/package validations and GitHub E2E compile-only all PASS. **Real two-instance Obsidian 1.14.4 encrypted GitHub E2E also PASS on the final D11 candidate**, bundle SHA-256 `fe91b08208ffd1a96468aa34a5a4d7d6dd9baa3ff536af2ccb6a49e0bdb03a68` deployed identically to disposable vault-e/vault-f. The test preflight re-verified numeric disposable repository ID `1284153873`; Force Push A, Force Pull B, update A, open-note veto and safe named Conflict modal B, then close and resume B all passed (`allCorrect:true`, `openEditorModalVisible:true`, `destructiveOverrideShown:false`, `cleanup:true`, exit 0). The unique GitHub test branch was deleted and absence verified; both managed Obsidian test processes were stopped. Never touched a personal vault. This is not a power-loss qualification.
- **Limitations:** This is a real child process terminated at a deterministic post-syscall boundary; **not** a power-cut or crash *inside* the OS rename syscall and does not prove fsync durability. Still unqualified: cross-process external file modifications and junction/symlink handle-pinning TOCTOU, synchronous rename latency on networked/slow drives, full 1,000-note native lifecycle on this SHA, actual macOS/Linux/mobile, C2 protocol and line merge UX. Do not merge/release; continue fail-first and ask before high-risk changes.

## 2026-10-22 Stage D9 — non-yielding Desktop rename critical section (audit only)

- **Base:** audit commit `2300083330b02b150411f4e1f07432380cfbcc7d`, isolated `.tmp/staged-production-6c67c8a` worktree. No GitHub Actions, no `master` edits/releases, no private vault, no credentialed GitHub E2E changes. User policy 1 on open editor and credential policy B both unchanged.
- **Finding:** D8 checked open editor immediately before `await fs.rename(stage,target)`. Asynchronous Node rename can yield back to Obsidian's renderer **after the guard** but before replacing the user's note, permitting a late editor/open-buffer race.
- **Fail-first:** Added D9 Recovery source critical-section contracts for both `commitStage` and `rollbackStage`. Each was RED against original asynchronous `await fs.rename` implementation. Supplements existing deterministic D8 guard-after-backup-rename and crash matrix tests; original JS CommonJS patch attempt did not intercept stable ESM named exports, so the test explicitly checks the non-yielding contract rather than claiming it injected a real OS syscall race.
- **Fix:** `src/lib/v4/platform-io.ts` preloads Node synchronous fs module **before** all critical work; the small **metadata-only** `renameSync(target,backup)` and `renameSync(stage,target)` pair run without awaited gaps after synchronous editor guards, with synchronous proof-gated original restoration if the second move fails. The `rollbackStage` target→residue, backup→target, and fallback residue→target renames also use `renameSync` after guards. Hashing, bounded content reads, stage bytes and GitHub transport remain async/unmodified. This prevents renderer JS reentrancy during the two-directory-entry swap; it is NOT a cross-process filesystem lock and may momentarily block Obsidian UI on unusually slow filesystems.
- **Gates:** Recovery (including both D9 source contracts, D8 editor-open-between-rename, D0/D2/D6 WAL crashes), Fast, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, Production Build, 100,000,000-byte bounded cryptographic soak, metadata, package, E2E compile PASS. Repeat Fast 10x completed successfully with exit 0.
- **Native Windows Obsidian 1.14.4:** Disposable profile `.tmp/native-obsidian-d6/profile-a`, vault `.tmp/native-obsidian-d6/vault-a`, plugin rebuilt from D9 code: dirty editor preserves unsaved text + original disk + staged bytes; clean open editor refused; staged file creation generated `create` watcher event and Obsidian/runtime matching bytes/index; closed file overwrite generated `modify` event with correct bytes/mtime/stage cleanup. All four CDP commands exit 0. Managed test app stopped; no personal vault.
- **Remaining release blockers:** Exact OS-level kill between synchronous renames; cross-process external editor/save race; nonlocal/network-FS UI latency; two-instance credentialed real-Obsidian sync including recovery and conflict modal acceptance; symlink TOCTOU, macOS/Linux/Mobile and coordinated C2 metadata/line merge UX. **Do not release solely on D9.**

## 2026-10-22 Stage E2 — durable GitHub rate-limit cooldown (audit branch only)

- **Scope / safety:** Worktree `.tmp/staged-production-6c67c8a`, branch `audit/2026-10-21-staged-production-integration`, based on E1 commit `feb25cb`. Never edit master or the private Zed working tree. No GitHub Actions or >100,000,000-byte test files. User policy B (the old pinned credentials finish the active Sync) unchanged.
- **Fail-first reproduced:** Added three `tests/v4/transport-policy.test.ts` cases: terminal HTTP 429 with `maxAttempts:1` had zero cooldown; `retryRateLimits:false` lost the cooldown; no scheduler deadline restoration API. All three failed on old scheduler and passed after fix. Fourth fail-first local storage test proved a failed disk write let a later request proceed without knowing persistence failed; now fails closed.
- **Engine:** `V4RequestScheduler` captures rate-limit deadlines even when retries are exhausted or disabled, offers `restoreCooldown`, rechecks deadlines after each policy sleep, and awaits an optional `onCooldown` storage callback before retry/return. Does not change mutation unknown-outcome or WAL policy.
- **Persistence:** Added `src/lib/v4/rate-limit-cooldown.ts`: 2 alternating SHA-256-checksummed JSON slots beneath `<configDir>/plugins/<pluginId>/v4-rate-limit-{0,1}.json`; only SHA-256 token fingerprint and epoch deadline. A new client loads deadlines before requests; a later client sees previous-token-client updates, shared across GitHub repos using the same token; distinct tokens remain isolated. Latest torn slot falls back to previous valid; both invalid fail closed. Disk write failures remain observable and block later calls rather than silently bypassing cooldown. All plugin-owned files are hard-excluded from V4 sync scope even if `syncPlugins` is enabled.
- **Regression:** `rate-limit-cooldown` 4/4, `transport-policy` 6/6, focused Settings/Runtime 37/37, Production Build, full Fast, 10x Repeat Fast, Recovery, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, virtual 100,000,000-byte soak, metadata and package validations and GitHub E2E bundle compile all **PASS**. **No live GitHub API 429 was intentionally forced**; rate-limit retry/persistence tested via controlled fake HTTP clock and persisted in-memory file adapter. E1's real GitHub 3/3 E2E suite (exit 0 with branch cleanup) was independently proven before E2.
- **Remaining release blockers:** Actual Obsidian Desktop multi-instance native app + editor save/crash/relaunch scenarios (especially staged files on Windows), hard symlink TOCTOU OS-handle gap, macOS/Linux/mobile physical tests, outstanding coordinated C2 protocol format decisions, line-by-line conflict merge UX, and independent security/performance audit. Do not release solely based on these automated gates.

## 2026-10-22 Stage E1 — server-enforced GitHub GraphQL beforeOid CAS (audit branch only)

- **Base:** `09a0e653a2e262a9b925ed5eed9c9d563b9cf9e0` in isolated audit worktree, never master. User policy **B remains binding**: a started Sync completes with its pinned connection/token/encryption settings; saving new settings does NOT cancel it. Never use GitHub Actions. Test payloads max 100,000,000 decimal bytes.
- **Fail-first:** Three new `tests/v4/github-transport.test.ts` cases proved `GitHubClient.updateGitRef` issued REST PATCH `force:false`, ignored expected HEAD, accepted a missing expected SHA, and had no GraphQL 200-errors fail-closed behavior. The old REST endpoint checks fast-forward ancestry, not exact old HEAD equality.
- **Fix:** For an existing branch, `updateGitRef` now requires full 40-hex before/after SHA; resolves and caches the repo GraphQL node ID on the starting GitHubClient; invokes `https://api.github.com/graphql` `updateRefs(input:{ repositoryId, refUpdates:[{ name:refs/heads/<branch>, beforeOid, afterOid, force:false }] })`. GitHub documents that `beforeOid` is enforced atomically by the server. Reject GraphQL errors even with HTTP 200, missing confirmation, invalid SHA or unavailable API; **NEVER fallback to REST PATCH**. The empty-branch create-ref bootstrap still uses REST POST (new ref creation is exclusive). The WAL/candidate reconciliation and reachable-ref unknown-outcome no-blind-retry policy remain unchanged.
- **Tests:** Focused transport 24/24 after fail-first 3/3, full Fast, Repeat Fast 10/10, Recovery, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, 100,000,000-byte virtual soak, metadata/package, GitHub E2E compile and Production Build PASS.
- **Live remote qualification:** Scoped target `diamond-crystal/test-vault-obsidian-encrypted-github-sync-multi-platform` is pinned to ID `1284153873` by project requirements. Current worktree has no `.env.github-e2e`, so E2E requires pointing `GITHUB_E2E_ENV_FILE` to the existing root file plus transient `GITHUB_E2E_EXPECTED_REPO_ID=1284153873`; never copy or log token. Live E2E was started with this setup, preflight accepted the environment and initial plaintext, conflict, out-of-band encrypted mutation scenarios passed. **Do not state whole-suite acceptance until terminal exit status and cleanup are checked.**
- **Live E2E final 2026-10-22:** First live run hit 600s runner deadline, second ended **RED** (2/3: encrypted stale/disjoint fresh C missed `B-local.md`, branch cleanup verified). We did not waive assertions. To separate GitHub eventual REST-ref visibility from actual record loss, live E2E now waits for the exact B-published HEAD before fresh C Force Pull; deterministic three-device bytes/path assertions remain unchanged. Also moved controlled external-branch-race injection to GraphQL `updateRefs` POST (the previous REST PATCH-only interception was obsolete). **Third live E2E completed exit 0** against pinned disposable repo `1284153873`: plaintext+encrypted baseline, disjoint stale edits including `B-local.md`, Copy conflict, rename/stale, delete/recreate, binary, externally mutated encrypted history and controlled beforeOid race all PASS. All three tests including `after()` branch cleanup completed successfully, so remote disposable branch cleanup is implied by test's fail-on-cleanup contract. Runner `edc143c3-49f4-4e82-98c6-62fc222ebfeb`. The prior red run remains recorded as evidence of a race to avoid recurrence; repeat native Obsidian qualification remains required.
- **Residual blockers:** E2 persistent rate-limit cooldown, actual Obsidian Desktop 2-instance/editor-save/kill/restart, platform symlink TOCTOU, Mobile/macOS/Linux and C2 remote metadata migration still need independent proof. GraphQL mutation may be denied on some credentials; fail closed and communicate the error rather than silently weakening concurrency safety.

## 2026-10-22 Stage D7 — immutable connection/encryption identity across an active Sync (audit only)

Sync no longer mixes new saved credentials/repository with its pre-existing WAL/publication transaction. No changes to stored data formats.

They do not change in the middle of a running attempt. Existing CAS tests previously failed under fully frozen policy; revised design preserves both safety and the existing semantics without suppressing tests.

Focused runtime/settings 37/37; full Fast, repeat Fast 10/10, Recovery, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, virtual bounded 100,000,000-byte soak, metadata/package and E2E-bundle compile, Production Build PASS. ## 2026-10-22 Stage D6 — Desktop small-file crash recovery through staged disk swap (audit branch only)

- **Base:** `a6ca20321ff3344fcb74441652c998457ac7585c`, audit worktree `.tmp/staged-production-6c67c8a`. User explicitly asked to continue with poteto-mode. NO GitHub Actions, master push/merge, tag/release, personal vault or generated test files above 100,000,000 decimal bytes.
- **Fail-first proof 1:** `tests/recovery/v4-crash-matrix.test.ts` demonstrated that a small (<32 MiB) WAL stage was written via `io.write` instead of staged commit; crash-after-swap and absent Desktop commit capability both failed before changes. Recovery now uses `commitStage` for any `preferStagedCommit` local I/O (Desktop with verified root), fails closed if unavailable, and preserves mobile and non-root fallback behavior. `createV4LocalIo` forwards the capability.
- **Real filesystem crash simulation:** Added 4 small-file cases to `tests/recovery/staged-crash-backup-orchestration.test.ts`: completed disk swap+receipt; crash after original moved to `.target-backup` before stage rename; crash after stage rename before receipt; changed target requiring manual review while preserving backup. Windows physical disk tests pass; these are deterministic fault injection, **not actual Obsidian.exe process kills**. Replacement is TWO renames + durable WAL repair, **not a single cross-file atomic OS transaction**.
- **Fail-first proof 2:** `tests/v4/runtime-desktop-staged-cache.test.ts` showed the Obsidian TFile cache can be stale while the staged target already exists and can be read from the filesystem. Desktop runtime now uses root-guarded `statFile`/`readWhole` to check actual target files; on Desktop the `getFiles()` listing is verified via bounded 4-concurrent direct stat calls instead of using potentially stale `TFile.stat`.
- **Fail-first proof 3:** A newly stage-committed file was absent from `getFiles()` until the Obsidian watcher caught up, so a subsequent scan could misclassify the file as deleted. Runtime tracks recently staged paths, merges not-yet-indexed paths into Desktop scans, and drops tracking when Obsidian's index recognizes a path. This tracker is in-memory and clears on plugin dispose; reboot/watcher behavior must still be tested in real Obsidian.
- **Verification:** Production Build PASS; focused recovery crash matrix 9/9, disk-backed orchestrations 8/8, Desktop cache/indexing simulation 1/1; full Fast, Repeat Fast 10/10, Recovery, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, 100,000,000-byte virtual hash soak, metadata/package validation and GitHub E2E bundle compile PASS after the final code changes. No credentialed GitHub mutation or real Obsidian GUI was run on this SHA.
- **Important limitations:** Direct filesystem swap bypasses the Obsidian Vault write queue and may interact with open editors/file-watchers in ways mock tests cannot prove. Do not call this production-ready until **two-instance native Obsidian tests** confirm reload/indexing, editor-save races, rename/create cases, crash/kill/restart, and reverse sync. Desktop staged replacement still has path-based symlink TOCTOU exposure, and the recently-staged path tracker is not persistent across process crash. Strict GitHub CAS/cooldown, config snapshot, legacy C2 format migration and mobile qualification remain pending.

## 2026-10-22 Stage D5 — local cache record integrity before trusting remote HEAD (audit only)

- **Base:** `86ef9ed` (Stage D4); changes isolated to `audit/2026-10-21-staged-production-integration`. No GitHub Actions, master, release, personal vault, or generated test files >100,000,000 decimal bytes.
- **Fail-first proof:** A saved local V4 index with valid matching header/shard `hash` labels but same-size tampering to `plaintextSha256` still loaded `remoteCommitSha` and was trusted as remote HEAD cache. New `tests/v4/local-index.test.ts` caught this; fixed test passes. The remote metadata/shard-hash contract is **unchanged**.
- **Local-only cache seal:** Each newly saved local shard includes a domain-separated SHA-256 `contentDigest` of `JSON.stringify(shard.records)`; `loadV4LocalIndex` checks the digest after parsing and before authorizing cached `remoteCommitSha`. Missing/invalid/different digest invalidates the entire local cache and forces full remote metadata reload; the index is a disposable cache, not a user-data source. This is an integrity check for accidental/stale/corrupt local cache, not a cryptographic authenticity guarantee against an attacker who can rewrite both file and digest. Older unsealed local cache is deliberately invalidated, with no remote history format changes.
- **Fixture compatibility:** An existing `tests/v4/settings-secrets.test.ts` runtime fixture manually forged a cached shard without any on-disk content checksum. Initial full Fast + Repeat detected 1/477 failure; test fixture now computes the valid local-only seal independently using Node SHA-256. After adjustment, focused settings 35/35, local index 11/11, full Fast 477/477, Repeat Fast 10/10 rounds, Recovery 58/58, Resource 11/11, Feasibility 132/132, virtual bounded 100,000,000-byte soak, metadata/package and GitHub E2E-compile, Production Build all PASS. No credentialed GitHub E2E or native Obsidian GUI test this stage.
- **Residual:** D4 still does not establish small-file `vault.modifyBinary` atomicity or restore a crashed partially-written file. Stage E strict CAS, configuration snapshot, native Obsidian crash E2E, C2 remote protocol migration and platform-specific containment remain separate gates.

## 2026-10-22 Stage D4 — original-content SHA for every WAL mutation (audit branch only)

- **Starting commit:** `db2d2e544d52222e24949a88a1dd9ccb6b574144` on `audit/2026-10-21-staged-production-integration`, isolated worktree. Never touch master, user/Zed root WIP, personal vault, GitHub Actions, or generated data above 100,000,000 decimal bytes.
- **Fail-first:** Added real `V4SyncSession` + `V4RecoveryStore` integration tests in `tests/recovery/v4-session-recovery-boundary.test.ts`. A same-byte-count/same-mtime local edit injected precisely after `remote-verified` WAL save was **incorrectly overwritten** by small-file Force Pull, or **incorrectly trashed** by Force Pull. Both new tests failed on prior implementation; both pass after fix.
- **Fix:** Every pre-existing target in a new WAL payload (small/large stage writes **and trash/rename trash**) now persists a SHA-256 original-content precondition. `pullPrecondition` uses hash already obtained during local scan; staged local push/merge uses the scanned `current.hash`; rename-trash uses prior record's hash. Before WAL save, hashes and size/mtime are re-verified against actual file bytes, with bounded streaming on large files, resident-budgeted whole-buffer read on small files, before/after stat checks and change rejection. Unknown-baseline conflict-copy targets acquire a hash by reading the existing file before WAL. Recovery executor already enforced nonempty `precondition.hash`; this patch makes it effective for all new WAL mutations. Legacy WAL missing hashes remain parseable (not yet a production migration policy).
- **Gates:** Fail-first reproduced 2/2 violations; fixed focused Recovery integration 4/4, Recovery suite 58/58, Fast, 10x Fast repeat, Resource 11/11, Feasibility, bounded virtual 100,000,000-byte crypto soak, metadata validation and production Build all PASS. `pnpm test:github-e2e:compile` and real Obsidian E2E not run this stage.
- **Remaining high-risk:** This does NOT make Obsidian `vault.modifyBinary` for small files atomic. Crash mid-small-file-write can still leave partial target without a durable original backup; must implement crash-safe writer or explicit user-review policy with native platform proof before production. Desktop native OS containment TOCTOU, untrusted local-cache integrity, mutable runtime configuration snapshot and GitHub strict CAS remain pending. An audit of file deletion receipt and stage ownership is also advisable. The new content revalidation increases disk reads on every WAL mutation; measure before release.

## 2026-10-22 Stage D3 Desktop I/O containment and staged-commit integrity (isolated audit only)

- **Base:** Continued from `f95d3763e6a2144c59c4d64fc5b91830486d7c18` in `audit/2026-10-21-staged-production-integration`; root/Zed WIP including untracked `AGENTS.md` preserved. Permanent policy: **NO GitHub Actions**, no master push/merge, release, personal vault, or test file > **100,000,000 decimal bytes**.
- **Fail-first proof:** Newly added `tests/v4/staged-desktop-io-containment.test.ts` exposed that Desktop bounded reading, staging write/append/removal and traversal path handling lacked containment enforcement. A symlink/junction could direct operations outside vault root. A separate fail-first test showed that the staged-commit catch path discarded the only staged payload on post-rename SHA mismatch.
- **Fix:** Reuse a vault-root-aware path validator for Desktop platform I/O. It rejects path escape, symlink/junction descendants, non-directory ancestors and realpath escape before Node reads, bounded file streaming, staging writes/append/remove and free-space probing. Existing stable roots are supplied by `createRuntimePlatformIo`. The compatibility harness without `desktopRootPath` retains legacy isolated low-level IO behavior, **not a claim of confinement without a root**. Desktop whole-file reads with a configured root no longer bypass the guard via adapter `readBinary`. The existing Desktop staged commit also rechecks all relevant paths after creating parent directories.
- **Staged-commit integrity:** The commit now validates staged content SHA-256 **before renaming** stage or user target. If a later failure occurs after stage-to-target rename, it preserves target/backup and reports a typed manual-review condition instead of blindly removing the target (which could contain a user edit). If the first rename succeeded but moving stage failed, it restores the original only with a proven backup hash and absent target. For large stages this adds an extra bounded disk hash pass, not additional whole-buffer memory. V4 WAL schema, remote pack/part format, encryption, GitHub writer, conflict policy and mobile semantics remain unchanged.
- **Verification:** New D3 focused tests **10/10**, including direct symlink/linked folder, traversal, legitimate IO, corrupt staging before replacement and prevention of deleting staged evidence; D2 backup/crash **14/14**; `pnpm build` PASS; Fast PASS, Repeat 10 Fast rounds PASS, Recovery **56/56**, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **132/132**, bounded **100,000,000-byte virtual SHA soak 1/1**, metadata/package validation PASS, three GitHub E2E bundles compile PASS. Local stage-only test files are tiny. No actual Obsidian desktop E2E or live GitHub E2E in this stage.
- **Known limitation:** Path-based symlink checks before filesystem calls reduce risk but are **not an atomic openat/handle-pinned proof against an adversarial TOCTOU swap**. A separate Windows/macOS/Linux native filesystem review and crash/restart acceptance is required. Do not claim production readiness; Stage C2 legacy protocol migration and Stage E strict GraphQL CAS/cooldown remain open.

## 2026-10-21 Stage D2 approved policy A — proof-gated desktop backup rollback (audit branch only)

- **Owner decision:** For an interrupted Desktop stage swap, auto-restore a user file **only when both original backup and expected staged target are provably identified**. If any evidence is absent or ambiguous, **preserve all available files, stop sync and ask the user**. No automatic unsafe conflict resolution. Owner approved this policy explicitly.
- **Scope:** Applied to isolated `audit/2026-10-21-staged-production-integration`, starting from `a4683e06967dded1fb7e9c0e86119f31912d6438`. No master push/merge, release, GitHub Actions, personal-vault access, or generated files >100,000,000 bytes; root Zed checkout and untracked `AGENTS.md` untouched.
- **WAL evidence:** New large staged replacement transactions persist an optional SHA-256 hash of the original target in the already encrypted V4 recovery payload before mutation; original is hashed with bounded streaming plus size/mtime rechecks. Reader accepts older schema-1 WAL without the optional hash, but **does not automatically restore historical backups lacking cryptographic evidence**. The shared local precondition also checks this original hash against actual contents, so same-size/same-mtime user edits fail closed.
- **Crash states:** `platform-io.ts` now offers proof-gated `rollbackStage` and `finalizeStage` with vault-root containment and symlink/junction checks. A private `.target-backup` is never deleted at the next commit start. Restoration requires expected original hash and size/mtime plus absence of conflicting user target or exact staged target SHA. During rollback the replacement is first **parked** as `.replacement-residue` rather than deleted, allowing a second crash to resume without discarding either copy. Residue-only/foreign residue/missing hash/corrupt backup/symlink paths require manual review. Finalizing after a durable receipt removes a backup only when original and new target hashes match.
- **Runtime integration:** `V4PluginRuntime` supplies vault root to the desktop platform, forwards rollback/finalize to the shared `V4LocalIo`, and raises a native Obsidian modal on ambiguous recovery. The modal supports leaving files untouched (default) and copying details; it deliberately has **no force-overwrite button**. `discardV4RecoveryStages` rolls back incomplete staged writes or finalizes completed ones **before** staged cleanup; a manual-review error halts cleanup and the sync run.
- **Tests:** Fail-first verified new Desktop rollback API was missing. Added `tests/v4/staged-desktop-backup-recovery.test.ts` (**14/14** including the linked-target-folder guard), `tests/recovery/staged-crash-backup-orchestration.test.ts` (**4/4**), and `tests/recovery/staged-backup-hash-precondition.test.ts` (**3/3**). After integration, Build PASS, Recovery **56/56**, Fast PASS, Repeat 10 successful Fast rounds, Resource **11/11**, Feasibility **132/132**, 100,000,000-byte **virtual cryptographic soak** PASS, metadata/package and GitHub E2E compile PASS. After the late linked-target-folder safety assertion, focused 14/14, Build and Recovery 56/56 passed again; full Fast/Repeat were rerun as final gates.
- **Remaining:** Still needs real Obsidian Desktop 2-instance encrypted/crash E2E on this exact SHA; symlink/TOCTOU and platform-level atomic restore independent review, real macOS/Linux/mobile acceptance, Stage C2 legacy shard hash/pack/part interoperability, Stage E strict GitHub GraphQL CAS/persistent cooldown, and release candidate UX. Do **not** declare production-ready or broadly merge candidate branch. Do not silently change handling of manual-review remnants.

Existing encrypted packed-member rename/preview and part tests remain in Fast.

## Landed work

### Publication race / conflict recovery

Key correctness properties now landed:
- publication-race retry is structured/evidence-based; stale-message regex classification is not used;
- conflict-copy reservation identity may survive a publication replan, but stale staged bytes do not;
- recovery generation ordering is authoritative before payload-key resolution;
- stale `replan-required` state terminalizes only after a successful fresh session and local index save;
- cancellation wins over transport/mutation errors when the operation signal is aborted;
- empty-repository/custom-branch bootstrap races replan the whole operation;
- encrypted speculative bootstrap retries derive the winner KDF before reading winner state;
- runtime publication mutation races are covered by production reconciliation tests;
- debug/error sanitization preserves an allowlisted cause chain while redacting sensitive values.

### Immutable Git read fallback

Key properties now landed:
- immutable commit-SHA Contents 404 fallback is path-directed;
- tree reads are non-recursive and descend only the exact path;
- positive exact entries may be used from truncated trees;
- absence is inferred only from complete (`truncated === false`) evidence;
- malformed/duplicate/unsupported evidence fails closed;
- executable blobs are supported;
- tree/gitlink final nodes mean file absence;
- managed symlinks are rejected;
- thrown and returned 404 forms are covered;
- no broad tree cache is retained.

### Local qualification / release and live-E2E safety

Key properties now landed:
- exact-SHA annotated qualification receipts;
- create-only stable ref ownership;
- explicit draft -> exact asset verification -> publish -> post-verify release state machine;
- deterministic dependency-free ZIP32 stored packaging;
- exact toolchain/version binding;
- canonical fetch/push origin checks;
- target repository identity pinned by numeric repository ID;
- target ID must differ from canonical/current source repository IDs;
- destructive target default branch is rejected;
- required local E2E config validates before source-repository network lookup;
- source/publication credentials are stripped from live-E2E child process boundaries;
- cleanup re-proves target identity before destructive branch operations;
- local release filesystem paths/assets reject unsafe symlink/non-regular-file states;
- ZIP entry traversal shapes are rejected;
- qualification temp refs are collision-safe and compare-deleted only when invocation-owned.

## Exact Node toolchain decision

The repository's official exact runtime was intentionally migrated from Node `v22.11.0` to Node `v24.11.0`.

- `.node-version` is the authority.
- qualification/release logic remains exact-version fail-closed;
- release/feasibility/V4 metadata tests were migrated to Node 24;
- CI/release workflows consume `.node-version` or the exact CI-produced version;
- `@types/node` was intentionally not changed during the runtime-only migration to avoid unrelated type/lockfile churn;
- final build and test evidence above proves the accepted runtime/type combination.

The earlier Windows/libuv failure under Node 24 exposed a real preflight-ordering defect: `scripts/run-github-e2e.mjs` attempted source-repository network lookup before rejecting missing required local E2E config. Commit `572a69f40a84e125375cbd9f23de9b11649a1896` fixed the ordering. The focused regression then passed on Node 24.

## Important invariants

- Never reintroduce regex/message-based stale-ref race classification where structured publication evidence exists.
- Publication reconciliation fails closed on incomplete ancestry/evidence.
- Do not treat staged conflict-copy bytes as authoritative across a changed remote snapshot.
- WAL recovery/discard remains the physical recovery-stage cleanup authority.
- Do not terminalize recovery state before local index persistence succeeds.
- Cancellation is canonical when the operation signal is aborted.
- Bootstrap races cause whole-operation replan.
- Recovery generation ordering precedes payload-key resolution.
- Immutable Git fallback never infers absence from truncated/incomplete tree evidence.
- Do not add a broad immutable tree cache without measured need.
- Destructive live-E2E operations must remain bound to explicit target numeric identity and safety checks.
- Release/qualification remains exact-toolchain and fail-closed.
